# Tasks

Live task board for Playarr: every tracked unit of work, its status, who picked it up and the evidence. `CHANGELOG.md` remains the permanent engineering log; this file is the current-work board. Open work comes first, grouped by workstream; finished work is collapsed under "Completed work" at the end.

Rules for editing: do not edit this file in a PR; add `tasks.d/<row-number>.md` fragments instead (see `tasks.d/README.md` and AGENTS.md, "Task tracking"). This repository is public: no environment data (host names, addresses, registries, node names) and no media titles in any row.

Steps only the repository owner can perform.

## 60. Legal pages and project conduct before public release

| ID | Task | Status | Owner | Branch | Depends | ETA | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10073 | Acceptable-use page: correct the "supplies no means of obtaining media" and "cannot get you a film you do not have" statements to match the Ombi/Seerr request integration | todo | | | | | Owner request 2026-10-11. `site/src/pages/legal/acceptable-use.astro` and the footer line in `site/src/components/Footer.astro` claim the server has no means of obtaining media, but `backend/crates/playarr-api/src/request_sync.rs` forwards user requests to a request manager the operator runs. Reword: Playarr forwards requests to a request manager you run and configure; any acquisition happens there, under the operator's responsibility; Playarr itself searches, indexes and downloads nothing. Keep the TickBox rationale. A disclaimer contradicted by the code must not ship. |
| 10074 | Privacy page and store privacy declarations: disclose Firebase Cloud Messaging and Google Cast on Android, and match Apple privacy labels and the Google Play Data safety form to the page | todo | | | | | Owner request 2026-10-11. `site/src/pages/legal/privacy.astro` says the apps contain no third-party libraries that send data, but the Android client ships `libs.firebase.messaging` (push token goes to Google) and a Cast sender. Add a section naming both, what Google receives and how to disable them; then fill the Apple privacy nutrition labels and the Play Data safety form from the page so the three agree. Depends on the request-manager wording in 60.10073 for the server section. |
| 10075 | Terms of use / EULA page for the apps and the site, linked from every store listing | todo | | | | | Owner request 2026-10-11. New `site/src/pages/legal/terms.astro` and footer link: software provided as-is under MIT (warranty disclaimer repeated in plain words), Playarr supplies no media and operates no service, the user is responsible for the legality of what they store, play and share, maintainers may refuse support per acceptable use, no revenue, no subscription, no data sold. Point `clients/ios/fastlane/metadata/en-GB/` (custom EULA field) and the Play listing at it. |
| 10076 | Marketing copy: remove "*arr stack" framing from the README highlights, site and every store listing; describe Playarr as a media server for your own library that integrates with library managers; keep Whisparr out of listings and behind an admin opt-in | todo | | | | | Owner request 2026-10-11. Scope: README.md highlights and "What this is", site copy, `clients/ios/fastlane/metadata/`, Play listing text and release notes. The integrations may still be named in integration docs. Whisparr: default off, admin opt-in with an age-rating note, never mentioned in store copy. |
| 10077 | Rights-holder and abuse contact: a named mailbox on the acceptable-use and privacy pages and in the store support URL | blocked_on_owner | Thomas | | | | Owner request 2026-10-11. Needed from the owner: the mailbox address to publish (a playarr.app alias is suggested) and confirmation it is monitored. Then agents add it to `site/src/pages/legal/acceptable-use.astro`, `privacy.astro`, `terms.astro` and the store support metadata, with a short "what to include in a complaint" paragraph. No US DMCA agent registration is needed (no hosted content), but a documented response channel is what rights holders and courts look at first. |
| 10078 | Support conduct: issue template config linking the acceptable-use page, a saved reply that closes acquisition questions, and one review-notes line stating the app reaches no third-party catalogue or streaming service | todo | | | | | Owner request 2026-10-11. Add `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/config.yml` with a contact link to the acceptable-use page and a "not a media source" blurb on the bug and feature templates; add the saved reply text to CONTRIBUTING.md; add one line to the App Review notes in `clients/ios/fastlane/metadata/en-GB/review_information/` and the Play review notes: the app accesses no third-party catalogues, download sources or streaming services (Apple guideline 5.2.3). |
| 10079 | Contributions and licence: Developer Certificate of Origin sign-off required, copyright line stays in the owner's name, third-party notices page kept current | todo | | | | | Owner request 2026-10-11. Add a DCO requirement (`Signed-off-by`) to CONTRIBUTING.md with a CI check on PRs; no CLA. Confirm `LICENSE` and `site/src/pages/legal/licences.astro` carry the owner's name only, and that the licences page lists every bundled third-party component and the TMDB attribution. |

| 10061 | Epic: move every store and marketplace listing from the company developer accounts to the owner's personal accounts before any public release | blocked_on_owner | Thomas | | | | Owner decision 2026-10-11: Playarr publishes under the owner's own name, not the company, so a store sanction or the *arr association never reaches the company's catalogue or clients. Closed testing currently runs on the company Apple and Google accounts. Needed from the owner: create each personal account (identity verification and fees are owner-only), then agents transfer or recreate the app records, re-mint signing material and rotate CI secrets per store (rows 59.10062 to 59.10071). Public submission rows (25.290 and any later store release row) depend on this epic. |
| 10062 | Apple App Store Connect: personal Apple Developer account; transfer or recreate the iOS, tvOS and macOS app record for bundle `app.playarr.ios`; new distribution certificate, iOS and tvOS provisioning profiles and App Store Connect API key | blocked_on_owner | Thomas | | | | Parent: 59.10061. Needed from the owner: enrol an individual Apple Developer account (seller name is the legal name) and grant the agents an API key. Then: request an App Store Connect app transfer from the company team; if Apple refuses a TestFlight-only record, delete it on the company team to free the bundle ID and recreate it on the personal team. Re-mint the distribution certificate, both provisioning profiles and the API key on the new team; update the `release-ios` environment secrets; revoke the Playarr certificate and API key on the company team; re-run the TestFlight workflow and confirm the external group (25.9701) still receives builds. Blocks 25.290. |

Verify closed testing and the TV track work from the new account. |

## 2. Server performance and security (2026-10-03)

| ID | Task | Status | Owner | Branch | Depends | ETA | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 9924 | Server: calendar API 5–22 s on a cold request | done | calendar-perf worker | calendar-api-perf | | | Landed via #462. Live after deploy: X-Calendar-Cache hit on all requests; day 0.7–1.9 s, week 1.6–2.1 s, month 1.6–2.6 s, agenda 92 d 1.7–2.8 s with a 1.47 s health RTT floor; cold week 7.8 s → 1.8 s. |
| 9930 | Native clients (Android, iOS, tvOS, Roku, Fire TV): port-less relay address tries 443, then falls back to 8484 | todo | | | 2.9921 | | Resumed 2026-10-11 (owner order, parity loop): parity-apple works the tvOS (Apple TV) part against live web; iOS part un-parked 2026-10-11: native hold lifted; not gated on web mobile validation. Apply the same 443→8484 fallback as row 2.9921. Back to todo 2026-10-11: parity-apple will not reach it today (tvOS focus is 17.470). |
| 363 | Server images built after the public-release scrub fail to start on SQLite databases: migration 15 checksum mismatch. | done | integrator | | | | Cause: the scrub reworded a comment in `0015_playlists.sql` (SQLite), so sqlx refused databases that had applied the old file. Fix: `run_migrations` rewrites the stored checksum for version 15 only when it equals one of four known comment-only earlier texts (the text both regional servers applied, the older rename-era text, and both as the planned history rewrite will produce them), then migrates; unknown mismatches still fail. The history rewrite rules change no migration file at main's tip. Evidence: copies of both regional databases migrated cleanly before the roll; both regional servers rolled, one at a time, to the fixed image (5 October 2026), version 15 reconciled, 0074 applied, health 200, no restarts. Rolling back to an image older than the unlock lease (row 31.115) is no longer possible (migration 0074 is applied); a server backup was taken on each server before the roll. Duplicate row: 445. Previous status: done: merged, live on both regional servers. |
| 9921 | Self-hosted servers do not answer on 443, but clients now default a bare public address to HTTPS on 443 (regression from PR 212) | done | relay-port worker | relay-port-fallback | 23.293 | | Landed via #471; relay-port-e2e 7/7 |
| 9935 | Server: peer sync still slow after row 9886: full read of a peer's availability rows takes 4-12 s per sync | done | peer-sync worker | peer-leaf-read | 7.9886 | | Landed via #481. Covering diff index plus content hash (migration 0083), one pooled read, chunked writes, per-peer lock, serve-stale own-availability cache. Steady-state re-measure 10 Oct 2026 03:30-04:05 UTC (about 35 min after 10:30 ICT, pods up 109 min on the #481 image), server-side durations from the request log, server A / B: `GET /api/v1/peer/availability` p50 0.50 / 0.34 s, p95 1.9 / 1.8 s, max 2.0 / 2.2 s (before: p50 0.65 / 0.73 s with p95 183 / 212 s and max 225 / 245 s, so the 180-250 s tail is gone). `POST /api/v1/peer/sync-push` p50 3.1 / 6.7 s, p95 16.4 / 32.8 s, max 33.7 / 37.3 s (before: p50 11.6 / 13.2 s, p95 28 / 31 s, max 37 / 103 s): better than before, still slow on B. Remaining slow statements: `SELECT media_file_id, content_hash FROM ...` (23 on A, 50 on B, p50 1.3-1.4 s) and `INSERT INTO media_file_language_state` (14 on A, p50 1.3 s), about 25-30 slow lines per hour. Follow-up needed for the content-hash read and the language-state writes. |
| 9937 | Home and Library cold load 3–6 s on live | done | home-load worker | home-cold-load | | | Landed via #510 (10 Oct 2026). Code done, Opus-reviewed with fixes applied (branch home-cold-load: new in-memory catalogue snapshot and cache keying). Cause: every cold Home scanned works, file ids and work/source pairs once per library and Library once more (no per-item N+1, but 3 full scans x 2 tables), on a CPU-starved server; resume plans cost one statement per episode. Fix: shared snapshot (catalog crate), snapshot-keyed rails cache, batched episode and file loads, On Deck details requested in parallel with resume plans. Statement counts pinned in `home_perf_tests.rs`. Remaining: a single Home request for On Deck (the details wave is still one round trip after progress). |
| 9952 | Peer sync-push still 3–7 s p50 / 33–37 s max; remaining slow diff read and language-state inserts | done | peer-sync-2 worker | peer-sync-2 | 2.9935 | | Follow-up to 2.9935 (landed via #481). Causes found: (1) the 1.3-3.8 s "slow" fingerprint read is the driver materialising 50,521 rows (covering index in use, about 0.2 s on an idle host), not the index or the write queue (queue commits average 3 ms, max 0.4-0.6 s); (2) the sender pushes the whole inventory every minute, 26 MB for 50,000 files, so a push is an upload plus about 3 s of CPU on a loaded pod (timed server-side, upload included); (3) language-state inserts are one direct transaction per file outside the write queue. Fix on branch peer-sync-2 (needs Opus review, DB concurrency): per-peer snapshot marker (migration 0084), push leaves the unchanged inventory out via a digest handshake (full resend every 10 min, older peers unaffected), batched language writes through the write queue. Opus review fixes applied (held digest recorded with the rows and computed by the receiver on push and pull, ack dropped on failed push, duplicate language entries merged, batch fallback). Awaiting the integration merge; live re-measure after rollout. Landed via #548. Live re-measure 2026-10-10 22:49 ICT, first 6 min after rollout of d41947c4 (n=6 per route per pod): POST sync-push p50 79 ms (pod A) / 36 ms (pod B), max 3.3 s / 2.0 s (was 3-7 s p50, 33-37 s max); GET peer/availability p50 1.3 s pod A with one 39 s first-after-restart call, 0.29 s pod B; slow-statement lines 3 on pod A (SELECT DISTINCT m.work_id, 1.4 s) and 0 on pod B. Sample is short, so re-check after an hour of steady state. |
| 9972 | Arr poller churn: repeated "new files" imports and runtime backfills every pass | done | arr-churn worker | arr-poll-churn | | | Live logs: same works backfilled every pass, and every restart re-imports multi-episode series Landed via #548. |
| 9973 | Works whose file rows never get language state (stale rows after file replacement?) | done | integrator | arr-stale-file-rows | 2.9972 | | Opus re-review fixes in progress: missing-row reconciliation, newest-progress wins, playback 404 for missing rows PR #593 merged 2026-10-10. Landed via #593 (merged 2026-10-11, a03d339f). Live 2026-10-11: Filters language list ready in about 1 s (API 0.8-0.9 s, 30 languages) at 1920 and 1280; arr reconciliation diffs 0/0/0 after deploy. |
| 10080 | Auth: refresh-token rotation must survive an app killed mid-refresh (persist before use; reuse-grace idempotency) | todo | | | | | Found 2026-10-11 while driving a real Roku. A client killed between the server rotating its refresh token and the client persisting the new one presents the retired token on the next start; beyond the 120 s reuse grace (`playarr-auth/src/refresh.rs`) the family is revoked and the device is signed out. Not reproduced for web logins: a fresh-device login and a login reusing another device id both left the Roku signed in; the store keeps one family per device id, revokes only on reuse detection or account deletion, and has no per-user session cap. Two hardening options. Client: write the new token pair to storage atomically before using it, and retry the refresh with the old token if the write did not happen. Server: inside the grace window return the same rotated pair for a retried old token (idempotent rotation) instead of rotating again or revoking. Applies to every client that refreshes at start-up. |

PR open: #1340. PR #1340 merged 2026-10-14. |

## 6. Web accessibility AAA (2026-10-09)

| ID | Task | Status | Owner | Branch | Depends | ETA | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 9860 | Web: focused-item details load without a backlog, are prefetched around the focus and cached (owner request, 9 October 2026) | done | web-integration worker | | | | New data module `FocusedDetails` (`lib/focusedDetails.ts`, `lib/detailsQueue.ts`) used by Library (Movies, Series, Music), Home and Search. One current request (high fetch priority, started at once and aborted when the focus moves on) plus a low-priority background lane of 2; `getWork` takes an `AbortSignal`, and `QueryCache.fetch` reference-counts callers so aborting a prefetch never cancels a request a screen is waiting for. After a 200 ms rest it prefetches 3 cards either side and the cards of the rows or rails above and below; an idle warm-up trickles in the rest of the visible items and waits while the remote is in use. A stored copy of any age renders at once and is revalidated. Library's left panel gains the movie runtime once the detail is stored. `scripts/nav-details-e2e.mjs` asserts key to details within a frame for prefetched neighbours, at most 1 current + 2 background requests while an arrow is held, and no stale title/runtime pairing; unit tests cover the queue, neighbour choice and cache. Server: the detail endpoint answers in about 215 ms (ETag, private max-age 30), so no server cache is added. Part 1 merged (#388: queue, prefetch, memory cache). Part 2, the IndexedDB copy (#417), is folded into #425. Row number 9860 was also used by the WCAG AAA work; that is now row 6.9890. Previous status: done (PR #425 merged 2026-10-09). |
| 9861 | Web: card artwork no longer queues ahead of the focused item's requests (owner request, 9 October 2026) | done | web-integration worker | | | | Card-sized artwork loads at low fetch priority, six at a time (#419, closed into #425). Backdrop and detail requests are not held back. Previous status: done (PR #425 merged 2026-10-09). |
| 9890 | WCAG 2.2 AAA across the web TV client and tokens, including the left details panel (owner requirement, 9 October 2026) | done | web-integration worker | | | | Text tokens 7:1 in both themes (#407). Per-area fixes for target size 44px, link names, text over images and focus appearance: settings and utility pages (#414), shell and library (#418), player and calendar (branch, no PR), all in #425. Replaces the AAA use of row number 9860, which clashed with the details row. Live watcher: AAA text-contrast fails dropped from 12 to 57 per page to 0 on home, calendar, playlists, settings, search, downloads, requests and watchlist. Previous status: done (PR #425 merged 2026-10-09). |
| 9891 | Web: card focus gets a subtle red/pink glow ring on top of the lift and shadow (owner decision, 9 October 2026; supersedes "no ring") | done | web-integration worker | | | | `--card-glow` ring in the shared card focus shadow and the art shadows; `focus-style-e2e` checks it in both themes and layouts (#425). ## 9. Android profile switcher and CI APK (2026-10-09)

| ID | Task | Status | Owner | Branch | Depends | ETA | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 9710 | Android (TV and phone): the profile chip opens the profile switcher with the account's profiles instead of an empty "Who's watching?" | done | android-profile-ci worker | | | | Owner-approved on 9 Oct 2026 despite the native parity hold. A QR-linked session stores its tokens first and learns its user id from a best-effort profile lookup afterwards; when that lookup was lost the session had a token but no identity, so the switcher listed nobody and offered only "Sign in". Release 0.3.0 has no later recovery, so the chip showed "Viewer" and the switcher was empty (reproduced on the TV emulator). Main recovers at start-up (PR 133), but a failed lookup there left the same state. The switcher now adopts the server's current profile before it lists (`adoptServerIdentityIfMissing`, `PlayarrProfileNameTest`). Fire TV and Roku findings are in the PR description. Previous status: done: merged (#356). |
| 9711 | CI builds and signs the Android (TV and phone) APK from every push to main and uploads it as a workflow artefact | done | android-profile-ci worker | | | | Owner request 9 Oct 2026: main produced no downloadable APK, so testers fell back to an old release or a local build showing 0.1.0 with versionCode 1. `ci.yml` job `android-apk` (main pushes and main dispatches only, GitHub-hosted runner, `release-android` environment signing secrets) builds `:app:assembleSideloadRelease`, verifies the signature and package with `verify-android-release-apk.sh`, and uploads `playarr-android-main-<version>-main.<commits>` (30 days). versionName `<version>-main.<commits>`; versionCode is the Play formula, above every release, so it installs over a release without uninstalling (`scripts/ci/android-main-version.sh`, tested by `test-android-main-version.sh`). Not published to Play. Pull requests stay affected-only through `android-check`; the job is part of the `ci-required` aggregate. Previous status: done: merged (#358). |

Previous status: done: merged (PR 300, superseded by the persisted cache of PR 309). |
| 9301 | Web player: progress is never written to the wrong item, unmount flush no longer fires on online/client change, offline queue has an overlap guard and drops poison messages, play() rejections handled, Space/K leave focused controls alone | done | web-progress-calendar | | | | Web TV audit P1, P3, P5, P6, B19. `lib/playbackProgress.ts` gates heartbeat and transition writes: engine state left over from the previous item after a media file change is ignored, and position 0 is never written. The unmount flush is keyed on the media file only. `lib/offlineProgressQueue.ts` collapses overlapping flushes, deletes mutations rejected with a permanent 4xx and never rejects. Space/K ignore buttons, links, options, dialogs and a minimised player. Unit tests with fake timers in `playbackProgress.test.ts` and `useGlobalMediaControls.test.ts`. Reconciled 2026-10-08: PR 298 merged, live on main. Unit tests `playbackProgress.test.ts`, `useGlobalMediaControls.test.ts` and the offline queue tests run in web CI. Previous status: done: merged (PR 298). |
| 9304 | Calendar: served from a background-refreshed, persisted source cache; users never see source errors; admin sees source freshness | done | web-progress-calendar | | | | Owner ruling 2026-10-08. `calendar_cache.rs` refreshes each Sonarr, Radarr, Lidarr and Readarr instance month by month (3 months back, 9 forward) every 10 minutes and on webhook or "Sync now", 30 s per call, doubling backoff to 30 min, stopping at the first failure; migration 0079 persists chunks and health so a restart serves at once. `GET /api/v1/calendar` only reads the cache (never waits, always `status: ok`, no error text); a failed refresh keeps the last good data. Admin Tasks shows "Calendar data: synced <time>" and the last refresh error per source (`calendar_last_success_at`, `calendar_error` on `sync-status`). Tests: serves while down, one timeout per dead source, backoff and due schedule, window, a slow source never delays a request, persistence round trip. Reconciled 2026-10-08: PR 309 merged, live on main (migration 0079, `calendar_cache.rs`, admin Tasks freshness line in `clients/tv-web/admin`). Previous status: done: merged (PR 309). |
| 9305 | Apply the "never wait on a source" rule to the other user-facing live *arr queries | done | backlog-resume | | | | Found while doing row 12.9304. (1) Discovery search and the request flow look titles up live in Radarr and Sonarr (`discovery.rs`, 5 s timeout) and wait for the answer. (2) Playback looks up Dubarr dub tracks per media path (`dubarr_audio.rs`), cached for a short TTL but a miss waits up to the request timeout. Request creation and admin health checks are interactive writes or admin tools and stay live. Background folder scans and the Ombi/Seerr request sync already run off the request path. Fix: serve both from a background-refreshed cache or a stale-while-revalidate cache with a deadline, and never show a source error to users. Done in the PR: `source_cache.rs` (stale-while-revalidate with deadline, single background refresh and failure back-off). Discovery search lookups (fresh 10 min, stale 24 h, 2.5 s miss deadline, cache cleared after a request is made) no longer return a "Could not reach" reason; Dubarr dub-track lookups (fresh 60 s, stale 24 h, 1.5 s miss deadline) never hold playback. Request creation stays live by design. Tests: `source_cache::tests`, `dubarr_audio::tests::slow_dubarr_never_holds_playback_and_fills_the_cache_afterwards`, `discovery::tests::{repeated_search_is_served_from_the_cache_without_asking_the_source,slow_request_provider_does_not_hold_the_search,unreachable_request_provider_degrades_without_failing_search}`. Previous status: done: merged (#344). |
| 9306 | Calendar refresh: allow a slow source 120 s per month and log the failure reason | done | web-progress-calendar | | | | Follow-up to row 12.9304. After the first deploy, the live Radarr and Sonarr missed the 30 s per-call budget (their health endpoint alone answers in 0.5 to 2 s, so the calendar query is far slower, consistent with Kestrel thread-pool starvation warnings in the Sonarr log). Nothing waits on a refresh, so the budget is now 120 s and the warning carries the reason. Reconciled 2026-10-08: PR 322 merged, live on main. Previous status: done: merged (PR 322). |
| 9307 | Calendar API: build per-entry actions faster | done | web-progress-calendar | | | | After row 12.9304 the live calendar answered from the cache without errors but still took 2 to 6 s (about 0.7 s of that is the link). Measured live: enrichment is 15 to 22 ms, per-entry actions are about 1.4 to 1.8 s whether the window holds 36 or 234 entries, so the cost scales with distinct works, not entries. `ResolveMemo` (merged) shares the household gate, backend and progress reads; this change reads each work's files concurrently instead of one after another and logs resolve and total time per request. Also per-viewer response cache (#348), periods cached and neighbours prefetched (#349), batch title lookups (#350). Previous status: done: merged (#327, #334, #343, #348, #349, #350). |
| 9303 | Web calendar: unobtrusive source note, today rolls over at midnight, rapid period presses keep every step, date-only releases keep their own date, agenda focus no longer rewrites the URL per key | done | web-progress-calendar | | | | Web TV audit R8 (client half), K4, K5, K22, K1. The failed-source banner that pushed the grid down 118 px is gone: users never see source errors (owner ruling; admin diagnostics live in Tasks, row 12.9304). `useToday` follows local midnight and wakes on visibility. `createAnchorStepper` builds rapid Previous/Next presses on the previous target. `releaseInstant` treats a midnight-UTC release as a date, so it stays on its day west of UTC and reads "All day". Agenda focus updates the details panel immediately and writes the URL after 250 ms rest; the filters memo ignores `selected`, so the list is no longer regrouped per key, and rows are memoised. Tests: `lib/calendar.test.ts` (fake timers, TZ cases), `pages/Calendar.test.tsx`; `smoke:calendar-nav` green. Reconciled 2026-10-08: PR 301 merged, live on main. Tests in `lib/calendar.test.ts` and `pages/Calendar.test.tsx`. Previous status: done: merged (PR 301). |

Unit tests in `WorkDetailViewModelTests`. Not verified locally: Apple builds run only on CI. Reconciled 2026-10-08: PR 201 merged: awaited exit flush under a background task, scene-phase flush, zero position never written; `WorkDetailViewModelTests` run in CI. Apple builds run only on CI. Unit tests in `TVPlayerProgressTests`. Not verified locally: Apple builds run only on CI; no real Apple TV. ## 14. QR sign-in code renewal (2026-10-07)

| ID | Task | Status | Owner | Branch | Depends | ETA | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 770 | Android TV QR sign-in shows "session expired" instead of renewing the code like web (owner report from a real Android TV); Fire TV QR screen shows an expiry error with a Try again button | done | android-qr-refresh | | | | Web reference (`DeviceLogin.tsx`, `hostedDeviceLink.ts`, `device-auth`): codes are capped at 5 minutes; on expiry (hosted 404 or deadline, server `expired_token`, any error whose message says "expired") the effect restarts with a fresh code and re-renders the QR with no error and no button; the hosted claim gets a 30 s redemption grace; `slow_down` adds 5 s; non-expiry errors (network, denied) show an error with Try again. Android: new `QrPairingFlow` (core-auth) renews on hosted expiry and on the server code expiring after approval (the reported "session expired" state), and goes beyond web by retrying network and HTTP blips with 2 s doubling to 30 s backoff, keeping the same code while it is valid; only an explicit denial shows an error. No server URL is ever requested on this path. The phone layout has no QR sign-in screen (credentials only), so only the TV layout changes. Fire TV: expired code now renews silently (same rule as web). Tests: 10 `QrPairingFlowTest` cases (expiry, server-code expiry, denial, blips, backoff cap), one Fire TV `LinkScreen` case. The recovery state (denial) now moves D-pad focus to the Try again button instead of leaving it on the theme dropdown in the stage chrome. Not run on an emulator: another worker's Playarr emulator was running for the whole session (one at a time rule), so the renewal is covered by the fake-driven unit tests and the focus change is compile-checked only. Follow-ups: rows 14.771-14.773. Reconciled 2026-10-08: PR 182 merged: `QrPairingFlow` in core-auth renews expired codes silently like web; Fire TV fixed with the same rule. Xbox and Harmony have the same bug in rows 14.771 and 14.772. Previous status: done: merged (PR 182). |
| 771 | Xbox: QR/device-code pairing parks on "The pairing code expired. Request a new code." instead of renewing the code silently like web | done | parity-xbox | | | 2026-10-11 22:00 ICT | Un-parked 2026-10-11 (owner order: run and test the Xbox UWP app on the Windows PC as wall slot 8, parity loop against web; 5.10001). Same bug as row 14.770. `XboxAppEnvironment.StartPairingAsync` turns `DeviceFlowException` (expired_token or deadline) into `PairingState.Failed`. Fix: loop to a new device code on expiry, keep error chrome for denial only, retry network blips with backoff; unit test over the pairing state machine. Needs the Windows toolchain. PR open: #769. The Xbox sign-in screen now shows the hosted-link QR and pairing code with no typed server address (typed address stays as "Sign in manually"); the code renews silently on expiry or a network blip (QrPairingFlow state machine with unit tests). Merged in #769. |

| 720 | Dispatch the cluster release gate from the release workflows instead of relying on a cron poll | done | Owner (Thomas) | | | | `release.yml` (after the GitHub Release is created) and `backend-release.yml` (stable `backend-v*` tags) send `repository_dispatch` `playarr-released` to the deployment repository; its cron becomes an hourly backstop. Mints a downscoped GitHub App token (`actions/create-github-app-token`, contents: write on the deployment repository only). Needs the owner to give the per-owner Ops App `contents: write` and add variable `OPS_APP_ID` and secret `OPS_APP_PRIVATE_KEY`; until then the steps log a notice and skip. Not verified end to end: needs the secret and a stable release. Reconciled 2026-10-08: PR 151 merged: `release.yml` and `backend-release.yml` dispatch `playarr-released` after a stable release (steps skip with a notice until configured). Remaining, owner only: give the Ops App `contents: write` on the deployment repository and add variable `OPS_APP_ID` and secret `OPS_APP_PRIVATE_KEY`; then verify end to end on the next stable release. Update 2026-10-08: the repository variable `OPS_APP_ID` and secret `OPS_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` exist (both set 2026-10-07), and the deployment repository gate already listens for `repository_dispatch` `playarr-released`. New manual workflow `ops-app-check.yml` mints a read-only token and reads the deployment repository without dispatching, to prove the pair and the App installation grant. Owner only if that check fails: the Ops App installation must allow `contents: write` on the deployment repository (needed for the dispatch) and the secret must hold that App's key. The first stable release then proves the dispatch. The check workflow now requests contents: write on the deployment repository and prints the granted permission levels (never the token), so it proves the write grant without dispatching. The check workflow requests contents: write on the deployment repository only (the mint fails unless the installation grants it) and never prints the token, so it proves the write grant without dispatching. Previous status: configured; awaiting end-to-end proof. |

Fix: `web-ci.yml` accepts upstream `push` or `workflow_dispatch` on main of this repository (same security conditions) and is callable; `release.yml` has a `web` job that deploys the release commit after the GitHub Release exists and fails the release on a failed deploy, the summary reports version and commit; `docs.yml` gains the `workflow_dispatch` trigger the train already used. Not verified: a live deploy (not run by the worker). Android Play upload still cannot be dispatched by the train (needs a version input). Reconciled 2026-10-08: PR 218 merged. Evidence: after the merge `Deploy Playarr Web` runs on main succeed (workflow_run, 2026-10-08), where 82 of the previous 100 were skipped. Previous status: done: merged (PR 218). |

| 10226 | Hisense VIDAA: verify Back from the focused Search input | done | parity-vidaa | hisense-search-back-10226 | | 2026-10-14 12:00 ICT | Input-mapping investigation complete, but Search Back navigation is NOT accepted. On the current TV debug build, guarded KEY_RETURNS on an empty, focused Search input produced only keydown/up Backspace/8; route remained /search, input stayed focused and empty, with no runtime or capture errors. VIDAA WebApp Development Technical Guide §5.2 maps mandatory Back/Return to VK_BACK_SPACE (keyCode 8); the shared handler deliberately leaves Backspace editing intact in text inputs. KEY_ESC produced no DOM event in the prior bounded probe; VIDAA keyCode 461 is handled by source but was not emitted in this capture. No claim about a physical-remote defect. This input limitation remains an acceptance gap for overall row 11.810. No source change, credential change, playback, or account switching. |

## 18. Public repository readiness (2026-10-05)

The repository is being prepared to become public: no environment data and no media titles anywhere (see AGENTS.md).

Link check and GFM render verified. Reconciled 2026-10-08: PR 64 merged: README banner, screenshot showcase and diagrams. The public screenshots were later retaken on the open-movie demo library by row 18.764 (PR 195). Previous status: done: merged (PR 64). |
| 361 | Remove media titles and real media artwork from the repository tree (owner order, 5 October 2026) | done | history-rewrite worker | | | | Owner order, 5 October 2026. Tree uses placeholder titles in tests, fixtures and docs, generated placeholder images and procedural Apple TV parity artwork. Item (1) done 2026-10-07: the fixture source stub now serves generated placeholder posters, backdrops and episode stills (`scripts/fixtures/art.mjs`). The eight README screenshots (added by PR 64 with openly licensed demo titles) were retaken too (the music album one became a calendar one) and the README screenshot attribution tables were removed. Site screenshots (movies library, series library, movie detail, server admin) were retaken at 1920x1080 from the web client and admin app against that library; the music screenshot was replaced by a series one because the fixture has no music library. The five Android Play screenshots (phone home, detail, playback at 1080x2400; TV home, detail at 1920x1080) were retaken from the native app on headless emulators signed in as a fixture user, replacing the ones that showed an openly licensed short film and its artwork. The social cards, feature graphic, TV banner and icon are brand graphics with no media and were left as they are. Item (2) done: the Apple TV references are native simulator captures of the built-in placeholder parity fixtures; new workflow `appletv-parity.yml` (macos-latest, workflow_dispatch plus a run when the file changes) captured all nine screens and uploads them as an artifact (nothing committed), and the web side of `scripts/appletv-parity-suite.mjs` now targets the fixture server with an optional fixture sign-in instead of the live site. Roku has no simulator: its references are real-device freezes compared with web captures by `clients/roku/scripts/parity_ae0.py`; the committed Roku images are UI chrome crops with no media, so there was nothing to regenerate, and new device freezes need a physical device. Done 2026-10-07: the retired Play review demo server, the last place an openly licensed short film was served, was deleted from the tree. **Public-image part reversed 7 Oct 2026 (owner):** README, site and store-listing screenshots must show the open-movie demo library with attribution, not the placeholder fixture; retaken by row 18.764. ## 19. Android player fixes (2026-10-07)

| ID | Task | Status | Owner | Branch | Depends | ETA | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 464 | Player quality menu shows "0 Mbps" for Original | done | android-player-bugs | | | | Root cause: the arr media analysis can report a zero video bitrate, stored as Some(0) and served as `quality_options[0].video_bitrate_bps`; the Android client then divided to whole Mbps. Server now ignores zero and derives the average from file size and duration (field unchanged: `video_bitrate_bps` on the `original` option), tests in `playback.rs`; Android shows "Original · 24.3 Mbps" or plain "Original". Web already hides a falsy bitrate but rounds to whole Mbps (`PlayerControls.tsx`); left to web-player-parity. Device check pending. Reconciled 2026-10-08: PR 50 merged: server ignores a zero bitrate and derives the average from size and duration (tests in `playback.rs`); Android shows the label. | 455 | Transcode admission slots: check whether node-local transcode slots leak when clients disconnect or a server pod restarts | done | transcode-slots-455 | | | | Audit of `playarr-transcode` admission: slots are an in-memory semaphore (not persisted, so a restart starts with every slot free; stale shared-cache session rows hold no slot), each permit is owned by the tracked ffmpeg child, and it is returned on expiry, on natural or failed ffmpeg exit (reaped before every admission), on spawn or store failure, on a playback Stop/Error event, and for abandoned clients by the idle reaper (15 s tick, idle TTL `PLAYARR_TRANSCODE_SESSION_IDLE_TTL_SECS`, default 60 s, already configurable). Regression tests in `playarr-transcode` pin each of those paths (abandoned client freed within TTL via the running reaper, failed ffmpeg frees at once, restart with stale cache rows, superseded/stopped session). One real leak found and fixed in `playarr-api` playback negotiation: a viewer replacing their own playback (quality or audio switch, retry after a decoder failure) spawned the new transcode before the old one was stopped, so with the default single slot the replacement got 503 `no_transcode_capacity` until the old one was reaped about a minute later (the row 20.260 symptom). The old transcode is now released before admission. Failing test first: `replacing_playback_hands_the_single_slot_to_the_new_transcode` returned 503 before the fix. Not reproduced: the held slot after killed browsers on a regional server (no access; the leak above or the up-to-75 s idle window is the likely explanation, to be confirmed after rollout). Reconciled 2026-10-08: PR 47 merged: the replaced transcode slot is released before admission (the one leak found in the audit); tests added. Previous status: done: merged (PR 47). |
| 456 | Headless browser playback harness: the web player stalls after the HLS manifest parse in headless Chromium against a local server while bare Shaka plays the same stream | done | Unassigned | | | | Not headless-only: an app bug, reproduced in Playwright Chromium (H.264 and AAC are supported there) with the built web client served by the fixture server. Bare Shaka with the same bearer filter played the same session in the same browser. Cause 1: the playback engine effect depended on the access-token provider, whose identity changed whenever the stored profile session list was re-created (every token fetch, including each segment request, re-read it from storage into a fresh equal array); the engine was destroyed and rebuilt repeatedly, and the load effect's `loadedForUrl` guard never loaded the new engine, so the page showed a spinner after the manifest and first segment. Fix: the engine reads the latest token provider through a ref, and the profile session update keeps the existing array when nothing changed. Cause 2 (found by the new reconnect scenario): after a backend restart the reconnect re-negotiated twice (the stale engine error was treated as a failure of the new session) and the terminal-state effect closed the replacement session, so its playlist 404ed forever; fixed with `isUnhandledEngineError` and `sessionCloseForEngineState` (unit-tested). Deliverable: `pnpm --filter @playarr-tv/web run test:playback-e2e` (`clients/tv-web/web/scripts/playback-e2e.mjs`): fixture user signs in through the form, plays a fixture film, asserts `currentTime` advances and the stream is set up once, SIGKILLs the fixture server mid-playback, asserts the inline Reconnecting card, restarts the server and asserts playback resumes past the stall point. Evidence 2026-10-07: 9/9 checks pass; vitest 775 pass. Before the fix the same script stalled at `currentTime` 0 with `readyState` 0. How to run: `docs/validation/fixture-environment.md`. Fixture clip length is now configurable (`PLAYARR_FIXTURE_CLIP_SECONDS`). Not covered: Firefox and WebKit, real devices, a server that stays down past the whole reconnect budget. |

| 260 | Emulator "decoding failed" on every title: investigation. | done | playback-verify worker | playback-aac-layout | | | Closed 2026-10-11 after live replay on servers running the PR 702 image (f65c34ee; web app from PR 653 and later). Cause was the AAC that ffmpeg encoded from a 5.1(side) source (DTS or E-AC-3): channel configuration 0 plus a PCE, which Chrome and the Android AAC decoder rejected. Fixed by PR 702 (fold audio with aformat=channel_layouts=5.1\|stereo\|mono before AAC encoding) on top of PR 653 (remux of a 1080p H.264 MKV, decode-error fallback, HEVC only where decodable). Replay results (video advances, frames differ, no decoder or pipeline errors, audio decodes): web (a) 1080p H.264 MKV with E-AC-3 5.1, remuxed, 25.7 s advanced, 26 distinct frames, 0.9 MB audio decoded, no errors; web (b) 4K HEVC Main 10 with DTS-HD MA, served as a 720p H.264 transcode, 26.9 s advanced, no errors, audio decoded; web (c) delegated HLS through the proxy with the test account's routing rule pointed at the other server, 29.2 s advanced, no errors, audio decoded; Android TV (b) on the emulator (debug build from main 3ec86db1): first frame after 16 s, 32 s of smooth playback with 0 dropped frames, 6-channel AAC decoded and the audio track reported started, no decoder errors, then one 30 s rebuffer because the CPU-limited node cannot hold a real-time 4K to 720p transcode alongside other sessions (a capacity matter, tracked under 20.10026 and 20.10012, not a decode fault). The routing rule 35e98783-16b4-433f-836d-e6663af0389e was set back to empty preferred nodes and verified on both servers (it stays as an inert rule because the admin API has no DELETE). Evidence: playback scratch folder, run3/. |

| 10023 | Tizen: Play shows 'Couldn't start this title / Failed to fetch' | done | parity-tizen-webos | | | | Investigated 2026-10-11 on the Samsung TV emulator with a debug build that logs every fetch, XHR and error to the host. The failures are not Tizen shell bugs. (1) "The server had a problem starting this title": GET /api/v1/playback/<file> returned 503 whenever web, webOS and Tizen started the same title as the same account at the same time (parity runs); the same request returned 200 when Tizen played alone, and the video played. (2) "Failed to fetch": a network-level rejection seen once on a catalog request just after a page reload; not reproduced on playback in 4 runs. Fixed on the way: remote media keys never registered (PR #674 merged). Server follow-up (503 on concurrent same-account playback) left to the coordinator. |
| 10026 | Playback negotiation returns 503 no_transcode_capacity when one account starts the same title on several devices at once | done | playback-verify worker | transcode-shared-10026 | 20.260 | | Reproduced live 2026-10-11 with three concurrent GET /api/v1/playback/<file> calls from one account on three device ids: two return 200 and the third 503 no_transcode_capacity (repeatable). Root cause: each device that cannot direct-play spawned its own ffmpeg through `reserve_capacity` in `playarr-transcode` (try_acquire on a node-local semaphore, `PLAYARR_TRANSCODE_MAX_CONCURRENT_JOBS`, 2 on the regional servers), and `release_replaced_transcodes` only frees the same user, device and file. Implemented on branch `transcode-shared-10026` (not merged: needs Opus review (streaming), which is unavailable until 17 Oct, hence the ETA): admission weighting (a slot is four permits, a video-copy remux takes one, a full re-encode four); a second playback session of the same account with the same file, profile, audio stream and start position attaches to the running transcode (reference count, ffmpeg stops with the last viewer; dubs never share); 503 now carries Retry-After, and the web player (10 retries, 2 s to 10 s) and the Android player retry with back-off instead of failing. Tests: transcode crate (weights, attach and refcount), playarr-api (three devices share one transcode and stop one by one, 503 plus Retry-After), vitest and Android unit tests. Review fixes (Fable): (H1) clients stop the old session before renegotiating, so a stop event that releases a linked transcode records the viewer (user, device, file) for 30 s and the renegotiation counts as admitted; (accepted limit) the 30 s grace also admits a restart of the same title by the same device; (M1b) a borrowed slot holds no permit and never touches the semaphore, so the pool cannot grow; (M1) a renegotiation by an already admitted viewer (seek, audio or quality switch, recovery of a dropped session) is never refused for capacity: when its old link was the last one the transcode is released first and its permits reused, and when others still share the old transcode the new spawn borrows one slot over the limit (the borrowed permits leave the pool when that transcode ends, so each admitted viewer holds at most one borrowed slot at a time; viewers without a running transcode are still refused); (M2) a per-key guard held across find, spawn and register makes racing first requests spawn once; (L1) a full peer's no_transcode_capacity is forwarded as 503 with Retry-After; the Android player now also retries capacity refusals on seek, switch and session recovery. The web seek, quality and audio switches also retry a capacity refusal with the same backoff and stay on the switching state. Not done: raising the slot count (do it only after review); step 4 of the proposal. Note: 4K to 720p real-time transcoding rebuffered once (30 s) on the current node capacity during the 20.260 replay, so a full re-encode still needs a whole slot. Merged as PR 743 (a176cd5a), live on both regional servers 2026-10-11 15:35 ICT. Live check: three concurrent playback-info requests from one account on three device ids for the same title (forced full transcode) all returned 200 with one shared transcode (same session URL); the first device then stopped and sought to 60 s and got 200 with a new transcode at source offset 60000 on the then-full node. |

| 292 | Server: rediscover unreachable peers after restart and use in-cluster TLS for server-to-server peer calls | done | peer-recovery | | | | Merged as PR 206 (`<image>`), rolled out through the pin in PR 209 (`<image>`). Live acceptance on both regional servers (4 October 2026):/Synced; both pods Running 1/1 with zero restarts; internal Service TLS verified with certificate validation on, both directions; unauthenticated peer account route returns 401 on both. Each region saw the other Active, and all five pull entity cursors (accounts, invites, libraries, availability, routing rules) reached the peer's `last_seen`. Sync logs show incoming account apply, safe isolation of a same-username conflict and no auth failures. Source-instance rows match by ID and `updated_at` across all records. The push cursor stayed stale and untouched (no freshness claimed). Open for the admin, not agent work: one pre-existing same-username collision keeps separate local accounts pending review. Tests: `push_fanout_skips_unreachable_and_left_peers`, peer-sync internal-route and DB recovery focused tests, fmt, clippy and CI passed. Previous status: done: merged (PR 206) and live. |
| 293 | Operations and clients: default public relay URLs to HTTPS on port 443 | done | | | | | Clients (web, Android, iOS, tvOS) normalise public IPv4 relay addresses to HTTPS with the port omitted, keeping explicit `:8484` and local addresses; merged as PR 212 (d9cfcdb1). The relay cut-over itself was finished earlier (PR 83); it was never an owner action, because the relay is DNS-only records pointing at each user's own server and Playarr hosts no servers. Checked 2026-10-10 against the two live test servers: both relay names resolve to the server's own address and answer HTTPS with a valid Let's Encrypt certificate on both 443 and 8484. That 443 answer comes from the cluster ingress in front of the test servers, not from the Playarr Server listener. A self-hosted server listens on 8484 only by default, so a bare address typed into a client now gets 443 and fails there: tracked in row 2.9921. Apple simulator acceptance is row 25.333. |
| 336 | Forward playback lifecycle events to the owning peer | done | session-event-forwarding | | | | Cross-peer negotiation creates the playback session on the owner, while clients post lifecycle events to the entry node. The entry node now forwards heartbeat and terminal events over a signed peer call to `POST /api/v1/peer/playback/sessions/{id}/events`; the owner checks the peer signature and that the session belongs to the forwarded account. Verified by route tests (entry forwarding binds the JWT user; owner route rejects bad signature, wrong user, unknown and departed peers). PR PR 260. |

| 458 | Regional server A logs a recurring "peer sync cycle failed on every known address" for one peer that has no known address: investigate whether the peer record is stale or its address is missing, and fix (clean up the record or stop retrying a peer with no address, and make the log say which) | done | peer-sync-458 | | | | Found while checking release v0.3.0. Related: 292. Root cause: a peer record with an empty address list (a member that never advertised one) still got a poller; every 60 s cycle hit the no-address branch, counted a failure (flipping the peer to Unreachable) and logged the generic "failed on every known address" warning. Both regional servers log it for the same peer record, so it is a record with no address, not a per-server fault. Fix: the poller now skips such a peer (no failure counted, stored status untouched) and logs a distinct "peer has no known address" warning naming the peer ID, at most once an hour; it syncs again as soon as membership sync supplies an address. Deliberately no automatic pruning: a record with no address may be a valid dial-out-only member, the repository has no delete, and marking it Left would gossip membership revocation to the whole group. Tests: `a_peer_with_no_addresses_is_skipped_without_counting_a_failure`, `a_peer_that_gains_an_address_is_synced_again`, two `no_address_log_gate_*` tests. Reconciled 2026-10-08: PR 42 merged: a peer with no known address is skipped without a failure count and logged once an hour; tests for skip, recovery and the rate limiter. Previous status: done: merged (PR 42). |

Rules (web is the reference): week and month tracks are two-dimensional (`data-tv-nav-geometric`): LEFT/RIGHT moves between days or cells by geometry, UP/DOWN between entries or weeks; before, web stepped through the DOM order of every entry on LEFT/RIGHT, which is why focus went up and down. Agenda: moving focus selects the entry, so the details follow without SELECT. The focused entry scrolls into every nested scroller; each scroller (agenda list, details panel, week columns and track, month grid) shows the rail edge fade (`.tv-scroll-edge-window`, clamp(28px, 4vh, 54px), opacity 0.58) on each side where content continues. Border: `--success` when `has_file` and (if the server listed actions) an enabled `play` or `resume`, `--ink-muted` otherwise; a series group is available only when every episode is; never the media kind or library. Tests: web vitest, `pnpm run smoke:calendar-nav` (Playwright, key sequences, scroll, fades, border colours in both themes), Android unit tests for the focus rules and availability, Compose UI tests with D-pad key sequences in `PlayarrCalendarTvNavUiTest`. | 197 | Dubarr direct-play alternate audio (remux or muxed rendition) without transcoding video | done | Unassigned | | | | Selecting a dub (or another source audio track) at original quality copies the source H.264 or HEVC video into fragmented-MP4 HLS and encodes only the audio, when the client reports the codec and the file fits its bitrate cap; otherwise the transcode path is unchanged. Verified 2026-10-07 against the local fixture server with `node scripts/fixtures/verify-dub-copy.mjs` (8/8 checks) as the unrestricted viewer: (1) H.264 client, `deu` dub, original quality: ffmpeg argv `-c:v copy -map 0:v:0 -map 1:a:0 -af apad -shortest -c:a aac -b:a 256k -hls_segment_type fmp4`, playlist `init.mp4` plus `.m4s`, ffprobe of the joined init and segments shows h264 320x180 plus aac, and the audio tone matches the dub file rather than the source tracks; the server logs "audio selection served with video copy". (2) HEVC title, HEVC-capable client, second source audio track: argv `-c:v copy -tag:v hvc1`, ffprobe shows hevc (tag hvc1) plus aac. (3) Client reporting no video codec, and a client with `max_bitrate_bps=1000`: both fall back to `-c:v libx264`. Gaps: only 320x180 fixture clips (a 600 s film was used for the long-run checks, not a real 4K remux or its real CPU saving); no player or emulator run; the multi-title checks used `PLAYARR_TRANSCODE_MAX_CONCURRENT_JOBS=4`. The sign-in screen does not itself say why the session ended; the expired-session text only appears inline when a screen receives a 401 before the session is dropped. Also fixed: a stored session with an access token but no refresh token (nothing can ever renew it) is now dropped instead of answering 401 forever with no refresh attempt, which is one way to get the reported pattern (401s on `/catalog`, no refresh call in the server log). The real-TV report itself was not reproduced; the app does attempt a refresh on every 401 when a refresh token is stored. Follow-ups done (server, PR followups-520): deleting a user renames the tombstone to `<name>~deleted~<id>` (migration 0077 renames older tombstones, sqlite and postgres) so the username is free again for `scripts/fixtures/up.sh`, and the admin delete revokes every refresh-token family of the user at once (API tests `a_deleted_username_can_be_registered_again` and `deleting_a_user_revokes_all_their_refresh_families`, plus db tests for the rename, the old-tombstone migration and the revoke). | 86 | Parallel range fetching and buffer tuning for 4K remux direct play on Android | done | android-eu-perf | | | | `ParallelRangeDataSource` (4 x 4 MiB concurrent ranges), load-control retune, `PlayarrPlaybackStats` logcat telemetry. Evidence: `:core-player:testDebugUnitTest` 11 tests, `:app:testSideloadDebugUnitTest` 144 tests, `:app:assembleSideloadRelease` and `:app:lintSideloadRelease` pass. Next: coordinator measures a library film from the emulator host against the test server. Merged in PR 39 . Validation 2026-10-05 on the the emulator host Android TV emulator against the test server, build from main, PlayarrPlaybackStats log tag. A 4K Dolby Vision remux (about 97 Mbps, the highest bitrate in the library; every title above about 45 Mbps is 4K HEVC): the emulator HEVC decoders (hardware and software) cannot decode 4K 10-bit, so playback fails at decode after the data path works; measured meanwhile bandwidth 17 to 81 Mbps (variable link), 7 to 8 parallel connections, 20 to 60 MB loaded before the decoder error. A 1080p H.264 title (about 15 Mbps) played cleanly: bandwidth about 100 Mbps, 8 connections during fill, 0 rebuffers, 0 dropped frames steady state. `scripts/scrub-test.sh` on that title: 9 of 10 steps seeked (the media fast-forward key step produced no seek on the emulator), seek_to_ready p50 4.0 s, p95 5.7 s, max 6.4 s, 0 rebuffers after seeks. Not validated: 4K remux decode, rebuffering at remux bitrate (needs a real device); the the test server link itself is below real time for 97 Mbps. ## Conventions



- One row per unit of work. Open work lives in the active sections; finished work moves to "Completed work".
- Format: one `## <N>. <Epic name>` heading per epic (N is a unique, stable positive integer: never renumber or reuse one; a new epic takes the highest + 1) and one table per epic, with exactly the header `| ID | Task | Status | Owner | Branch | Depends | ETA | Notes |`. Cells are not padded. A literal pipe inside a cell is written `\|`.
- Status values: `todo`, `in_progress`, `in_review`, `blocked`, `blocked_on_owner`, `done`. A row that is `done` carries its evidence, `won't do` or `won't fix` reason in Notes. A `todo` row is free to pick up; `blocked_on_owner` waits for a decision or an action only the owner can take.
- `Owner` names a concrete session, subagent or tracked task; `Unassigned` means free to pick up. List handoffs with an arrow.
- `Branch` is the git branch doing the row's work when it is known (named in Notes or Owner, or a worktree or open PR matches the row); otherwise empty. Never invent one.
- A task's reference is `<epic>.<task>` (for example `3.9926`); the ID column keeps the bare ID. Name tasks by reference in Depends, Notes, commits, PR titles and bodies, chat and reports.
- `Depends` lists the references (`<epic>.<task>`, comma-separated) of the rows a row waits for. `ETA` is `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM <timezone>` (an abbreviation such as ICT, or an offset such as +07:00), or empty.
- Rows converted from the former five-column board (`# | Task | Status | Picked up by | Notes`) keep the old free-text status at the end of Notes as `Previous status: ...`.
- Evidence is a PR link, test result or a short live-verification note. Keep long logs in the PR, not here.
- Never record environment data (host names, IP addresses, registries, node names, paths) or real media titles in a row.
- Add or change rows with `tasks.d/<row-number>.md` fragments; the merge train folds them in.
